  sorry but i couldn't get it .. what's the difference between spare & downtime ?
    ↳ **@LittleCheekyGuy** (👍7 · 2017-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0lHCemUta1w&lc=Ugha0XNxg-9p3HgCoAEC.8K57KGyW6kG8LnccBpvp3d
      Spare is something that is extra. For example, Daniel could have spare clothes. This means that you have more of something. You can use this in a sentence like: 'I have spare clothes that you could wear'. It could be to help someone where you can provide them with something, or for no reason.
      
      Downtime means the extra time you have to relax, enjoy yourself or do something different. It is usually the time after school or work - like weekends. For example, you could say: "I dance in my downtime".
      
      Overall, you could say: "Would you like to dance in our downtime? I have spare shoes for you if you don't have some."

  thx😀 but since "merely" means either "just" or "only", on which occasions should we use "merely" instead of "only"/"just"?
    ↳ **@ivetterodriguez1994** (👍0 · 2020-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z1PauShJx8c&lc=Ugwdg_kmDB35UKY9Nw54AaABAg.8kJWlFHWWS694BazaIQOZx
      "Mere" or "merely" means "solely" or "only" typically implying that something is insignificant, trivial, or excusable. "Just" is the word you would hear the most in spoken language. Only is typically used less than "just" but is interchangeable for the most part.

**@عربيجزاءري** (👍1 · 2017-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kPT3IBoFIaA&lc=UghrYod03tvG3HgCoAEC
  quelqu'un peut m'expliquer en français la différence entre remeber et recall
    ↳ **@Anglaide** (👍0 · 2019-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kPT3IBoFIaA&lc=UghrYod03tvG3HgCoAEC.8TbdMHjSYss8rYX2pz0u-K
      Ce sont des synonymes.  La différence entre les deux autres est plus importante.  "Remind" veut dire "rappeler" et se fait à quelqu'un d'autre (ex. "Remind me to pay the bill") . Remember" veut dire "se rappeler" et est fait par soi-même (ex. "I didn't remember to pay the bill").  Est-ce plus clair ?

**@PavelTverdunov** (👍0 · 2013-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kPT3IBoFIaA&lc=Ugyb1hhaixuXbP3FBBh4AaABAg

**@Dude-iz2dw** (👍0 · 2019-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kPT3IBoFIaA&lc=UgzbOb5T-RG0aKh5vSd4AaABAg
  when do we use "I am remembering"?
    ↳ **@Anglaide** (👍0 · 2019-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kPT3IBoFIaA&lc=UgzbOb5T-RG0aKh5vSd4AaABAg.8qSvnxiugaU8rYXKBE5Iz0
      We don't.  "Remember" is a verb of the mind and is considered non-progressive, i.e. it cannot be in progress.  You either remember or you don't.  The only exception might be if you had amnesia and things were slowly coming back to you.  But even then it would sound weird.

  "Off" phrasal verbs are the most difficult for a French speaker, because there's no equivalent in French of the preposition "off". For example, "nous buvons dans un verre", "nous mangeons dans une assiette", whereas you drink off a glass and eat off a plate. So, I'll have to watch you video over and over again till I don't even have to think about the meaning of those f**** "off" phrasal verbs.
  Thank you for the quality of your work.

      Try focusing on only one aspect at a time e.g. If you want to practice a new word, just make sure that one word is correct in your sentence. You can worry about the rest of it later. Same goes for grammar: If you're practicing the present perfect tense, make sure you get the tense exactly right and worry about the vocabulary later. Trying to do too much at once can be overwhelming.

**@Asiro-S** (👍82 · 2020-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GEQhDeNyM8s&lc=UgwiAiA7y1KmHcuK1gh4AaABAg
  1) Don't think, just listen - You can't understand 2 conversations at once.
   2) Ask questions - Engage to be engaged.
   3) Home Practice - 
    a. No subtitles
    b. 2-3 sentences
    c. 10x - for beginners/ 3x 
    d. Then play sub
    e.  Write down sentences you hear to know what you're missing and then practice what you've missed.
   4) Close your eyes and listen.
    a. It will make easier to listen for you, because when you lose one sense (vision in our case), another one's getting better(hearing).
   5) Order to watch movies:
    a. For kids
    b. Stupid comedy
    c. Action Movies
    d. Drama
    e. Dark comedy

      That is because reading is a process that adapts to your understanding speed. You understand what you read because you read as slowly as your brain can understand the words in a sentence. But when you listen to someone speak, he or she is probably speaking faster than the speed you can process the words. The solution...practice listening over and over again. It will get better only with practice. But if you do not practice...forget it. It is never gonna happen.

      Even native speakers work to increase their vocabulary. Our vocabulary is tested every year in school, especially in high school as we get ready to apply for college/university. The key is to learn phrases and remember situations. For example, I gave you these: a creepy feeling, an intriguing question, an entertaining movie. "Entertaining" means the same as "good" when we talk about movies, but we can't say "an entertaining student" to mean "a good student." Right?

**@nawarnawar5822** (👍1 · 2019-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3bJo2t8_dic&lc=Ugxiy4GruCFNc4IG-YR4AaABAg
  Hi Jennifer, thanks for brilliant lessons you always make.
  I have two points that i would like to ask you about
  The first point is that there are many teachers on youtube  who advise us to learn the synonyms of any word we lean, but frankly i don't find this way useful, because sometimes synonyms of the word wouldn't always make sense, if we wanted to replace the word in use by its synonyms.
  I'm not talking about your way, which is definitely perfect.
  The second point is that i noticed that you put a hyphen between two words in order to make a compound adjective. To be honest with you, i watched a lot of videos about compound adjectives, but the idea isn't clear enough. I hope that you may make a lesson about compound adjectives, because your ways of teaching are always understandable, and i trust you a lot.
    ↳ **@Englishwithjennifer [channel author]** (👍2 · 2019-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3bJo2t8_dic&lc=Ugxiy4GruCFNc4IG-YR4AaABAg.8skY0Q8LTZE8ske91AJCSl
      Hi. If you're able to join the live stream on Monday, please do. I'll show examples of how to choose appropriate synonyms. See my reply to Haider above, too. You need to be very aware of associations a word has, and you need to pay attention to context. How do people use those synonyms? Think of the phrases I've given you:
      an intriguing question
      a thought-provoking story
      a creepy feeling
      hilarious scenes
      etc.
      Learning phrases will help more than memorizing single words. 
      
      Compound modifiers/adjectives usually require a hyphen when we use a present or past participle:
      the strangest-looking thing
      a thought-provoking story
      a time-honored tradition
      a well-built car
      
      This is a handout for teachers, but you may use it for self-study. :) https://englishwithjennifer.files.wordpress.com/2014/03/finding-four-leaf-clovers_handout1.pdf
      
      I'll note the request for a lesson on compound words/compound modifiers.

      Most apologize and ask for the person's name: I'm so sorry. I forgot your first name. (Kathy). - - Kathy! That's right. So sorry.
